# Elements of Biology: question papers in full

180 unique papers · 2019-20 to 2025-26 · Computer Engineering, Data Science, Mechanical, Civil, Information Technology, Common (all branches), Artificial Intelligence, Mechatronics, AI and Data Science, Electronics and Telecommunication, Computer Science, Cyber Security, AI and Machine Learning, Computer Science and Business Systems

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

````text
SVKMs NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCH.OF TECH.MGMT ENGG-NM-M
````
````text
Programme: B. Tech (CE)/B. Tech (CIVIL)/B. Tech (CS)/B. Tech (CSE - CS)/B. Tech - (CSEDS)/B. Tech
(DS)/B. Tech (EXTC)/B. Tech (IT)/B. Tech (MECH)/B. Tech (MXTC)
Year: I/Semester I/Semester III (Exam Year: 2024-2025)
Subject: Elements of Biology
Date: 06 Dec 2024   Time: 02:00 pm - 05:00 pm (03:00 Hrs.)
Max Marks: 100
FINAL EXAMINATION(2024-2025)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 3 pages
2. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page
3. Figure in right hand side indicates full marks
4. Question No.1 is compulsory
5. Out of remaining questions, attempt any 4 questions
6. In all, 5 questions to be attempted
7. All questions carry equal marks
8. Assume suitable data if necessary
1.   20
a. What design inspired by the kingfisher is used in the Shinkansen Series 500 train, and what are   4
its contributions to speed and energy efficiency?
b. Explain the process of protein denaturation, highlighting the factors that cause it and its effects   4
on protein structure and function. Also, draw and describe the building blocks of proteins.
c. How essential is catalysis for life, and what makes enzymes valuable as biocatalysts?   4
d. Explain the concepts of anabolic and catabolic reactions, including examples for each. Also,   4
describe how these reactions are connected to metabolism.
e. Define microbial biotechnology and provide one example of its application.   4
2.   (Attempt Any 4 Questions)   80
a.   20
Page 1 of 3
--- page 2 ---
i. What is Golden rice, and how does it exemplify the potential benefits of biotechnology?   5
ii. Using the case study of DNA data storage, what are the key principles of biomimicry   10
applied in engineering, and how does this lead to more sustainable and efficient data
storage systems?
iii. Classify proteins based on their roles and functions, explaining any five specific   5
categories.
b.   20
i. Describe any two biomolecules, including their types and functions, to show how   10
biomolecules form the foundation of life. Include illustrations and specific examples to
support your explanation.
ii. Explain the following genetic terminology, providing examples where applicable:   10
1. Genotype and phenotype
2. Homozygous dominant alleles
3. Epistasis
4. Genes
5. Gene mapping
c.   20
i. Case Study: Discuss the development of a nanoparticle-based vaccine delivery system   10
for cancer immunotherapy. Highlight the design, mechanisms of action, and potential
benefits of this approach.
ii. Provide a brief description of each stage in cellular respiration, starting with the   10
oxidation of pyruvate and concluding with the electron transport chain. Illustrate the
main products and byproducts at each step using a flowchart or diagram.
d.   20
i. The feather type in a particular bird species is controlled by two pairs of genes.   10
Dominant gene T produces tufted feathers, while its absence results in smooth feathers.
Gene P produces vibrant feather coloring in the presence of T; however, it cannot
express without it (in tt), resulting in plain-colored feathers. How can a Punnett square
analysis be used to predict the genotypic and phenotypic ratios of offspring from a
cross between two birds: a male (PpTt) and a female (PpTt)? Additionally, specify the
type of gene interactions involved in this scenario.
Page 2 of 3
--- page 3 ---
ii. Describe the mechanism by which enzymes catalyze reactions, using a suitable   10
diagram and an activation energy plot. What does the Michaelis-Menten equation
represent, and how does it explain the relationship between substrate concentration and
reaction rate? Include a plot to demonstrate this relationship.
e.   20
i. How does transcription function in gene expression, and what key enzymes and   10
molecules convert DNA into RNA and, ultimately, into protein through transcription
and translation? Include a supporting diagram.
ii. With the help of a diagram, explain microbial growth kinetics with a focus on the   10
phases of bacterial growth. How do environmental factors influence microbial growth?
f.   20
i. What characterizes dominant autosomal single-gene disorders? Also, explain an   5
example outlining their inheritance patterns with Punnett squares.
ii. Describe the lock-and-key model of enzyme action briefly, and provide a supporting   5
diagram to illustrate this concept.
iii. Provide a concise overview of bioenergetics and explain how endergonic and exergonic   5
reactions differ in terms of energy flow.
iv. How do the energy and carbon utilization strategies of autotrophs and heterotrophs   5
differ?
Page 3 of 3
````

````text
1.   20
a. What design inspired by the kingfisher is used in the Shinkansen Series 500 train, and what are   4
its contributions to speed and energy efficiency?
b. Explain the process of protein denaturation, highlighting the factors that cause it and its effects   4
on protein structure and function. Also, draw and describe the building blocks of proteins.
c. How essential is catalysis for life, and what makes enzymes valuable as biocatalysts?   4
d. Explain the concepts of anabolic and catabolic reactions, including examples for each. Also,   4
describe how these reactions are connected to metabolism.
e. Define microbial biotechnology and provide one example of its application.   4
2.   (Attempt Any 4 Questions)   80
a.   20
Page 1 of 3
--- page 2 ---
i. What is Golden rice, and how does it exemplify the potential benefits of biotechnology?   5
ii. Using the case study of DNA data storage, what are the key principles of biomimicry   10
applied in engineering, and how does this lead to more sustainable and efficient data
storage systems?
iii. Classify proteins based on their roles and functions, explaining any five specific   5
categories.
b.   20
i. Describe any two biomolecules, including their types and functions, to show how   10
biomolecules form the foundation of life. Include illustrations and specific examples to
support your explanation.
ii. Explain the following genetic terminology, providing examples where applicable:   10
1. Genotype and phenotype
2. Homozygous dominant alleles
3. Epistasis
4. Genes
5. Gene mapping
c.   20
i. Case Study: Discuss the development of a nanoparticle-based vaccine delivery system   10
for cancer immunotherapy. Highlight the design, mechanisms of action, and potential
benefits of this approach.
ii. Provide a brief description of each stage in cellular respiration, starting with the   10
oxidation of pyruvate and concluding with the electron transport chain. Illustrate the
main products and byproducts at each step using a flowchart or diagram.
d.   20
i. The feather type in a particular bird species is controlled by two pairs of genes.   10
Dominant gene T produces tufted feathers, while its absence results in smooth feathers.
Gene P produces vibrant feather coloring in the presence of T; however, it cannot
express without it (in tt), resulting in plain-colored feathers. How can a Punnett square
analysis be used to predict the genotypic and phenotypic ratios of offspring from a
cross between two birds: a male (PpTt) and a female (PpTt)? Additionally, specify the
type of gene interactions involved in this scenario.
Page 2 of 3
--- page 3 ---
ii. Describe the mechanism by which enzymes catalyze reactions, using a suitable   10
diagram and an activation energy plot. What does the Michaelis-Menten equation
represent, and how does it explain the relationship between substrate concentration and
reaction rate? Include a plot to demonstrate this relationship.
e.   20
i. How does transcription function in gene expression, and what key enzymes and   10
molecules convert DNA into RNA and, ultimately, into protein through transcription
and translation? Include a supporting diagram.
ii. With the help of a diagram, explain microbial growth kinetics with a focus on the   10
phases of bacterial growth. How do environmental factors influence microbial growth?
f.   20
i. What characterizes dominant autosomal single-gene disorders? Also, explain an   5
example outlining their inheritance patterns with Punnett squares.
ii. Describe the lock-and-key model of enzyme action briefly, and provide a supporting   5
diagram to illustrate this concept.
iii. Provide a concise overview of bioenergetics and explain how endergonic and exergonic   5
reactions differ in terms of energy flow.
iv. How do the energy and carbon utilization strategies of autotrophs and heterotrophs   5
differ?
````

````text
i. The fin shape of a particular fish species is controlled by two pairs of genes. Dominant   10
gene F produces long fins, while its absence results in short fins. Gene C produces a
colorful pattern in the presence of F; however, it cannot be expressed without it (in ff),
resulting in plain fins. How can a Punnett square analysis be used to predict the
genotypic and phenotypic ratios of offspring from a cross between two fish: a male
(CcFf) and a female (CcFf)? Additionally, specify the type of gene interactions
involved in this scenario.

inhibition? Differentiate between competitive and un-competitive inhibition, providing
examples and supporting diagrams.

the key enzymes involved, accompanied by a diagram of the process.

in bacterial growth, and why it is essential for bacterial cells to progress through these
phases.

along with at least one disease associated with each type.
````
